## 5. Critical Operational Notes
- **Secrets**: all in root-readable 0600 files on VM114/CT906 under `/etc/llm-manager/secrets/` — NEVER print, log, or commit. Node root password on CT906 at `~/.miam_root_pass`; VM114 SSH key `~/.ssh/id_ed25519` (NOT `.miam_vm_id_ed25519`).
- **This workstation (hermes-jordan, CT906 @ 10.0.20.195)**: nodes reachable at their **10.0.20.x** addresses (NOT 10.0.10.x — that space timed out this session). Use paramiko with root password for node ops; `ethtool -m/-e` for transceiver reads.
- **Perftest**: `-x N` for GID index (space form rejected); `-c` = connection type; both sides must use same RoCEv2 GID type; server needs `setsid` to survive.
- **RoCEv2 GID indexes**: 111 mlx5_0=1, mlx5_1=3; 144 mlx5_0=3. Each port differs — match per-port.
- **NCCL test pattern**: torchrun, MASTER on one guest, join addr other guest's 172.31.0.x; `NCCL_DEBUG=INFO`; success = `ALLREDUCE_OK True` + `NET/IB` in logs.
- **Emporia API**: `channels` is a dict keyed by channel_num string; 1MIN scale → kWh/min.
- **PG is SQL_ASCII** — ASCII-only literals. GLM/qwen3 are thinking models: empty content + `finish_reason: length` under small caps is expected.
- **Nginx pitfall**: never park config backups in `sites-enabled/` (duplicate default_server breaks reload) — use `/etc/nginx/backups/`.
- **Plan doc**: `MARION-IA-USA_LLM_Manager_Implementation_Plan_v0.2.1.md` is the spec of record; §19.8 (RDMA qualification gate) and §9.3 (caps-before-fallback) are hard gates enforced in code.


We are continuing work on Proxmox servers. Please help by reading up on this handoff document. I will also give you the implementation plan that we are executing, too, and the architecture spreadsheet in the next two steer messages.

# MARION-IA-USA LLM Manager — Implementation Plan v0.2.1

**Status:** Design decisions incorporated; electricity-cost composition corrected; ready for read-only infrastructure preflight before deployment scripting  
**Decision date:** 2026-09-08  
**Target Proxmox host:** `MIAM-00135`  
**Candidate VMID:** `108` — **must be proven unused before creation**  
**Candidate IP:** `10.0.20.108` — **must be proven unused before assignment**  
**DNS name:** `llm-manager.marion-ia-usa.internal`  
**Primary purpose:** Central control plane, OpenAI-compatible API gateway, Hermes-agent key authority, routing layer, benchmarking system, observability system, and cost/performance database for the MARION-IA-USA local LLM infrastructure.

---

## 1. Executive objective

Create a dedicated VM named:

```text
MARION-IA-USA LLM Manager
```

on `MIAM-00135`, initially bootstrapped from the known-good GPU Driver Installer controller VM architecture but fully re-identified and stripped of copied GDI secrets/state.

The LLM Manager will become the stable point through which local Hermes agents and other authorized internal clients use LLM inference. It should:

- monitor and control vLLM deployments on `MIAM-00111`, `MIAM-00112`, `MIAM-00143`, and `MIAM-00144`
- issue a distinct API key to each Hermes agent
- expose a stable OpenAI-compatible endpoint
- route requests to healthy local deployments first
- wait a deliberate, configurable interval for local capacity instead of prematurely buying cloud inference
- fall back to OpenRouter only when policy permits and local capacity/health criteria are not satisfied
- enforce OpenRouter spend caps and per-agent policy
- benchmark model/topology combinations and keep historical results
- collect GPU, vLLM, request, health, and power telemetry
- calculate energy cost and performance-per-dollar
- use GPU onboard telemetry as the primary dynamic local power signal
- ingest Emporia whole-circuit/PDU/mini-split data as an independent facility-level measurement source
- store operational metadata and history in PostgreSQL
- offload high-volume or full prompt/response history away from the constrained `MIAM-00135` system disk
- provide controlled vLLM service lifecycle actions and bounded VM recovery actions
- provide a future path for multi-node vLLM deployments using the 100 Gb interconnect between `MIAM-00111`, `MIAM-00143`, and `MIAM-00144`
- eventually compare/rank local inference, OpenRouter, direct APIs, and other officially supported provider integrations by availability, quality, cost, performance, context, privacy, and policy

The LLM Manager is a **control plane and gateway**. It does not normally host model weights or perform GPU inference itself.

---

## 2. Decisions locked in v0.2

The following answers from Section 28 of v0.1 are now treated as design decisions.

| Item | v0.2 decision |
|---|---|
| VM identity | Candidate `VMID 108`, candidate `10.0.20.108`; fail closed unless both are proven unused |
| Gateway | LiteLLM Proxy plus a Startup Teams custom management/control web application |
| Client keys | One distinct API key per Hermes agent |
| Qwen 3.6 normal load | Design around about 10 concurrent agents/requests, with an initial hard concurrency envelope of about 20 pending benchmark qualification |
| Routing default | Local first, deliberate wait/queue, OpenRouter fallback only if the key policy allows it |
| Cloud policy | Default OpenRouter master/aggregate budget must have hard caps; individual keys may override fallback permissions within administrator limits |
| VM control | Manager may control both vLLM service lifecycle and bounded inference-VM recovery/restart |
| Power | GPU telemetry first; modeled CPU/RAM/platform overhead; Emporia used as independent whole-circuit/facility telemetry |
| Electricity planning rate | Rate 400 base energy (`$0.09992/kWh` Sep-May; `$0.13398/kWh` Jun-Aug) + year-round Emporia-observed variable fees/riders adder of `$0.05830/kWh`; effective planning rates `$0.15822/kWh` Sep-May and `$0.19228/kWh` Jun-Aug (displayed as 15.82¢ and 19.23¢) |
| Logging | Metadata-first initially; architecture must support full prompt/response logs later with off-host storage |
| Admin auth | Existing LDAP/LLDAP identity system; local break-glass/bootstrap admin only if needed |
| DNS | `llm-manager.marion-ia-usa.internal` |
| Database | PostgreSQL; application designed so PostgreSQL can reside on a future storage-oriented VM on `MIAM-00147` |
| Fallback timing | Local requests are given meaningful time to queue/run; slow local TTFT alone is not a reason to immediately duplicate the request in OpenRouter |
| Distributed inference | 100 Gb multi-node path is a first-class future capability but remains unroutable until independently qualified |

### Important interpretation: API keys versus concurrency

The number of issued API keys should **not** be limited to 20. API keys identify clients; concurrency consumes model capacity.

For the known `Qwen3.6-35B-A3B-AWQ` deployment, start with a deployment profile such as:

```yaml
recommended_concurrency: 10
initial_hard_concurrency_limit: 20
max_issued_agent_keys: unlimited_by_model_capacity
```

The concurrency values must be model/deployment specific. A different model, quantization, topology, context target, or GPU set may receive a different admission envelope.

---

## 3. Known inference baseline

### 3.1 MIAM-00111 qualified baseline

Known qualified environment:

```text
Host:             MIAM-00111
Proxmox:          VE 9.2.10
Inference VM:     VM103 jordatech-vllm-test
Guest:            Ubuntu 24.04.4 LTS
Kernel:           6.8.0-139-generic
GPUs:             6 x RTX 3080 20 GB
Aggregate VRAM:   120 GB
NVIDIA driver:    R580 open, module 580.173.02
Python env:       /opt/vllm-venv
API port:         8000
Model:            QuantTrio/Qwen3.6-35B-A3B-AWQ
```

Working topology:

```text
TP1 / DP6 / EP6
```

Core flags:

```text
--tensor-parallel-size 1
--data-parallel-size 6
--enable-expert-parallel
--expert-placement-strategy linear
--quantization awq
--dtype half
--gpu-memory-utilization 0.92
--kv-cache-dtype turboquant_k8v4
--attention-backend TURBOQUANT
--enable-prefix-caching
--enable-chunked-prefill
--scheduler-reserve-full-isl
--watermark 0.05
--kv-cache-metrics
```

Validated large-context operating point:

```text
10 concurrent requests
229,376 input tokens per request
+ 256 output tokens per request
```

Do not record the synthetic 250k test as a qualified model limit because tokenizer expansion exceeded the declared 262,144-token model maximum.

Representative 4096-in/256-out throughput results:

| Concurrency | Output tok/s | Total tok/s | Mean TTFT |
|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| 10 | 249.85 | 4259.49 | 4.27 s |
| 16 | 271.21 | 4623.65 | 4.73 s |
| 20 | 311.98 | 5318.69 | 5.20 s |
| 32 | 319.69 | 5450.14 | 7.39 s |
| 48 | 348.62 | 5943.41 | 11.13 s |

For normal Hermes service, concurrency 10 remains the initial preferred baseline even though aggregate throughput rises at higher concurrency.

### 3.2 MIAM-00112

Known major difference:

```text
6 x RTX 5060 Ti 16 GB
AMD Threadripper Pro 3955WX
128 GB RAM
```

Do not copy the MIAM-00111 topology without qualification. Its 96 GB aggregate VRAM and different GPU architecture require a separate model-fit and benchmark envelope.

### 3.3 MIAM-00143 and MIAM-00144

These are intended to reproduce or complement the MIAM-00111 inference stack, but each must independently inventory:

```text
GPU model/count/VRAM
PCI BDFs
IOMMU groups
kernel
NVIDIA driver/module packages
inference VM resources
100 Gb NICs/ports
network addresses
RDMA capability
GPU-to-NIC PCIe topology
```

Never copy PCI BDFs between hosts.

## 5. Base VM and bootstrap strategy

### 5.1 Candidate VM sizing

Start with:

```text
VMID:             108 candidate only
IP:               10.0.20.108 candidate only
Hostname:         marion-ia-usa-llm-manager
Display name:     MARION-IA-USA LLM Manager
OS:               Ubuntu Server 24.04 LTS
vCPU:             8
RAM:              16 GB
System disk:      64 GB
Local data disk:  64-128 GB only if actually available/needed
Machine:          q35
Firmware:         OVMF
QEMU agent:       enabled
Ballooning:       disabled
Autostart:        enabled after acceptance test
GPU passthrough:  none
```

Because `MIAM-00135` has constrained storage, do not assume a large local telemetry/database disk. Keep all high-volume paths configurable and relocatable.

### 5.2 Clone source

Use the existing controller:

```text
VM107
gpu-driver-installer-v4-1
10.0.20.107:8443
```

as a **bootstrap image only**.

Required clone hygiene:

1. Full-clone while source is in a consistent state.
2. Keep clone network-isolated until identity reset is complete.
3. Change VM display name and guest hostname.
4. Regenerate machine-id.
5. Regenerate SSH host keys.
6. Remove copied application secrets, private keys, audit records, state, tokens, and TLS identity.
7. Remove GDI application code/services not needed by LLM Manager.
8. Retain only useful base hardening, QEMU guest agent, service patterns, reverse-proxy/TLS patterns, and OS configuration.
9. Generate a new TLS certificate for `llm-manager.marion-ia-usa.internal`.
10. Run a secret scan before enabling the new network interface.

### 5.3 Fail-closed VMID/IP preflight

Before any clone or network mutation, the deployment script must prove both identifiers are unused.

VMID validation should include at least:

```text
cluster-wide Proxmox resource lookup for VMID 108
MIAM-00135 qm list
/etc/pve configuration search
```

IP validation should include at least:

```text
existing Proxmox guest configuration search
DNS lookup
neighbor/ARP table lookup
ICMP probe
ARP duplicate-address detection from the correct VLAN
DHCP/static reservation inspection where available
```

A failed probe does **not** prove an IP is unused. Any contradictory or ambiguous signal aborts deployment.

---

## 6. Software stack

### 6.1 Gateway

Use **LiteLLM Proxy** as the provider-normalization and virtual-key gateway.

Reasons:

- OpenAI-compatible API surface
- virtual keys
- authentication/authorization hooks
- spend tracking and budgets
- rate limiting
- provider normalization
- retries/fallbacks
- local OpenAI-compatible/vLLM endpoints
- OpenRouter and future external provider adapters

The custom Startup Teams control application remains authoritative for infrastructure-specific logic such as vLLM lifecycle, GPU health, power, benchmark qualification, host maintenance, and MARION-specific routing eligibility.

### 6.2 Custom control application

Recommended:

```text
Python 3.12
FastAPI
Uvicorn/Gunicorn
SQLAlchemy
Alembic
PostgreSQL
systemd
Nginx or Caddy
```

Suggested layout:

```text
/opt/llm-manager/
    app/
    control/
    collectors/
    routing/
    benchmark/
    auth/
    power/
    providers/
    distributed/
    migrations/
    static/
    templates/

/etc/llm-manager/
    manager.yaml
    nodes.yaml
    models.yaml
    routes.yaml
    tls/
    secrets/

/var/lib/llm-manager/
    cache/
    benchmark-artifacts/
    exports/
    spool/

/var/log/llm-manager/
```

### 6.3 Service separation

Use separate systemd units where practical:

```text
llm-manager-web.service
llm-manager-collector.service
llm-manager-benchmark.service
llm-manager-control.service
litellm.service
```

Do not let a hung benchmark worker take down the gateway.

---

## 7. Network, DNS, TLS, and access

Expose one HTTPS name:

```text
https://llm-manager.marion-ia-usa.internal/
```

Recommended routing:

```text
/admin      -> custom admin UI
/api        -> manager orchestration API
/v1         -> LiteLLM/OpenAI-compatible API gateway
/healthz    -> manager health
```

Access policy:

```text
LAN:             allowed
Tailscale:       allowed where policy permits
Public Internet: denied
```

Hermes clients should only require:

```bash
OPENAI_BASE_URL=https://llm-manager.marion-ia-usa.internal/v1
OPENAI_API_KEY=<agent-specific-key>
OPENAI_MODEL=startupteams/general
```

They should never require:

- SSH access to inference servers
- Proxmox credentials
- vLLM host credentials
- OpenRouter master credentials
- direct provider master credentials

---

## 8. Admin authentication and authorization

The web UI should authenticate against the existing LDAP/LLDAP environment.

Recommended roles:

```text
LLM-Viewer
LLM-Operator
LLM-Admin
LLM-Security-Admin
```

Example permissions:

| Capability | Viewer | Operator | Admin | Security Admin |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| View status/metrics | Yes | Yes | Yes | Yes |
| Run benchmark | No | Yes | Yes | Yes |
| Drain/undrain | No | Yes | Yes | Yes |
| Restart vLLM service | No | Yes | Yes | Yes |
| Restart inference VM | No | Restricted | Yes | Yes |
| Edit deployment config | No | No | Yes | Yes |
| Create/rotate agent keys | No | No | Yes | Yes |
| Change provider master secrets | No | No | No | Yes |
| Change global spend caps | No | No | Yes | Yes |

A local bootstrap/break-glass administrator may exist for installation/recovery, but LDAP should be the routine administrative identity source.

**Secret handling:** do not hardcode or reproduce the root password, LDAP bind password, Emporia password, OpenRouter key, or LiteLLM master key in this plan, source control, shell history, or generated configuration. Inject them through root-readable secret files or a later secret manager.

---

## 9. Hermes API-key and policy model

### 9.1 One key per Hermes agent

Structure:

```text
Organization
  -> Project/service
      -> Hermes agent
          -> one or more rotating API credentials
```

Each stored key record should include:

```text
key_id
key_prefix
hash
agent_id
project_id
created_at
expires_at optional
enabled
allowed_logical_models
max_concurrency
requests_per_minute optional
tokens_per_minute optional
daily_token_budget optional
monthly_token_budget optional
daily_dollar_budget optional
monthly_dollar_budget optional
local_allowed
cloud_allowed
fallback_policy
provider_allowlist optional
logging_policy
```

Raw keys are shown only at creation/rotation.

### 9.2 Initial policy profiles

Support at least:

```text
LOCAL_ONLY
LOCAL_FIRST_OPENROUTER_FALLBACK
CLOUD_ALLOWED_EXPLICIT
SPECIFIC_MODEL_ONLY
COST_CAPPED
BENCHMARK_ONLY
```

Recommended default for normal Hermes agents:

```text
LOCAL_FIRST_OPENROUTER_FALLBACK
```

but only after OpenRouter global caps are configured.

### 9.3 OpenRouter budget controls

Before enabling fallback for any default/general key:

```text
[ ] global daily spend cap configured
[ ] global monthly spend cap configured
[ ] warning threshold configured
[ ] hard-stop threshold configured
[ ] per-project cap supported
[ ] per-agent cap supported
[ ] audit event generated on fallback
[ ] actual OpenRouter reported cost stored when available
```

Fallback should never silently convert an uncapped local workload into uncapped external spend.

---

## 10. Routing and admission policy

### 10.1 Default sequence

For a normal general-purpose Hermes request:

```text
1. Resolve logical model and agent policy.
2. Find healthy local deployments that satisfy context/capability constraints.
3. Apply deployment admission limits.
4. If local capacity is temporarily full, queue for the configured local patience interval.
5. If admitted locally, let the local request continue even if TTFT is slow.
6. Do not start a duplicate OpenRouter request merely because local TTFT is slower than expected.
7. If no local deployment becomes admissible before the queue deadline, and the key permits cloud fallback, use OpenRouter.
8. If local execution actually fails after dispatch and a retry is safe, apply the configured retry/fallback policy.
9. If cloud fallback is disallowed or budget-capped, return an explicit unavailable/capacity response.
```

### 10.2 Initial timeout model

Use separate timeouts for separate failure modes.

Recommended starting configuration, to be benchmark-tuned:

```yaml
routing:
  local_queue_wait_seconds: 120
  local_connection_timeout_seconds: 15
  local_first_token_timeout_seconds: 300
  local_total_request_timeout_seconds: 1200
  cloud_fallback_on_queue_timeout: true
  cloud_fallback_on_slow_ttft_alone: false
  cloud_fallback_on_connection_failure: true
  cloud_fallback_on_local_5xx: policy_controlled
```

The values are intentionally generous because the objective is to use local inference when it is functioning, not optimize for minimum latency at any cost.

Per-model values should replace these defaults once sufficient history exists.

### 10.3 Capacity-aware admission

Every deployment stores a qualification envelope:

```text
qualified_max_context_per_request
qualified_concurrency
qualified_aggregate_context
recommended_concurrency
hard_concurrency_limit
KV cache dtype
GPU memory utilization target
preemptio

*(truncated, 52258 chars total)*

LDAP/SSO, budgets UI, benchmarking, power/cost after that.\n\nOnce the user authorizes a build (\"continue until finished with all stages\"), keep working the roadmap in one session where possible; deferred work (multi-node/RDMA rings, Emporia telemetry) gets explicitly SCOPED into the handoff doc — never silently dropped.\n\n## Architecture\n\n```\nHermes agents / internal clients\n        |  OPENAI_BASE_URL=http://<gateway>/v1  + per-agent virtual key\n        v\nnginx (/v1 → LiteLLM :4000, /admin /api → FastAPI :8001)\n        v                          v\nLiteLLM proxy (virtual keys,     FastAPI control plane\nbudgets, load-balance, fallback)  (health probes, key mgmt UI,\n        v                          deployment control)\nPostgreSQL (litellm + app DBs on a dedicated CT/VM)\n        v\nvLLM fleet (per-node VMs, one port 8000 each; TP/DP/EP per GPU set)\n```\n\n## Deployment recipes\n\n- `references/litellm-deployment.md` — full LiteLLM proxy bring-up on Ubuntu 24.04 with PostgreSQL backing: dependency chain (prisma, nodejs), schema generate/push, P1010 and pg_hba fixes, systemd unit, nginx routing, key lifecycle API calls.\n- `templates/litellm_config.yaml` — known-good multi-endpoint gateway config (local vLLM only, no cloud fallback).\n- `templates/vllm-systemd.service` — vLLM unit template.\n- For node/guest/SSH layer: `proxmox-cluster-infrastructure` skill, esp. `references/pve-guest-vm-operations.md` and its `scripts/ssh_run.py`.\n- `scripts/bench_engine.py` — benchmark engine (streaming TTFT, C1–C48 presets, required-Qwen workload, RESULT_JSON output). See SKILL.md §Benchmarking.\n- `scripts/pve_ops.py` — allowlisted Proxmox VM ops via API token (status/start/shutdown/reboot; fixed IP→(node,vmid) map). See SKILL.md §Bounded VM-level control.\n- `references/spend-analytics.md` — LiteLLM_SpendLogs / VerificationToken schema facts, OpenRouter-style analytics endpoint pattern, energy-attribution math (v0.6.0-v0.7.1).\n- For the 100 Gb / RDMA / multi-node workstream: `references/rdma-ring-qualification.md` — RDMA-0 inventory results, the cold-port bring-up gotcha, offline .deb delivery pipeline, and open RDMA-1..4 gates.\n- **Static-IP selection for new guests (proven 2026-09-10):** ICMP sweep alone is NOT sufficient — an offline host's IP looks free. Cross-check THREE sources: (1) `/etc/pve/corosync.conf` ring0_addr list = the definitive node mgmt IP map (nodes own whole number bands like .110-.119, .133-.135, .143-.149), (2) Kea reservation CSV (`/api/kea/dhcpv4/download_reservations` — md5-verified CSV, OPNsense web-session API), (3) ping+`arping -c2 -I vmbr0`. The user corrected a near-miss: MIAM-00110 was OFFLINE so .110 pinged dead but belongs to the node (user: \"10.0.20.110 is definitely used as a static IP for MIAM-00110\"). DHCP pool .190-.250 is off-limits for statics; pick the lowest free gap (e.g. .187), set it via container netplan, then add a Kea reservation (`/api/kea/dhcpv4/add_reservation/` with `{reservation: {subnet: <uuid>, ip_address, hw_address, hostname, description}}` — subnet uuid from `search_reservation/` rows) so the pool can't lease it later.\n- **OPNsense scripted access (no SSH, no API keys):** web-session login works — POST /index.php with ALL hidden CSRF inputs + `usernamefld`/`passwordfld` + `login=1`, then `X-CSRFToken` header (regex from any /ui page JS: `setRequestHeader\\(\"X-CSRFToken\",\\s*\"([^\"]+)\"`) for `/api/...` MVC calls. Kea lease search: `/api/kea/leases4/search/`; reservations: `/api/kea/dhcpv4/search_reservation/` / `add_reservation/`. Login page must be fetched from a vantage that can reach 10.0.10.1:80 (the Proxmox nodes can; CT906 cannot). Full recipe: proxmox skill `references/opnsense-unbound-dns.md`.\n\n## User preferences embedded (2026-09)\n\n- **\"Fix what you create; don't leave broken artifacts\"** — when the user asks to review\n  or fix problems, they expect actual repairs + verification, not a findings report\n  (\"fix any problems that you've created\"). Prefer static IPs for infrastructure roles,\n  but if an address choice is uncertain, **fall back to DHCP rather than risk a\n  collision** (\"Use DHCP if you are confused instead of static IP assignment\") —\n  ambiguity on IP assignment is the one case where DHCP is the user-endorsed default.\n- **Write-functional core first** — monitoring-only dashboards disappoint; keys + completions + model control are the deliverable (see Golden rule).\n- **Scope, don't drop, deferred work** — the user explicitly approves working through all plan stages in one continuous run; anything genuinely deferred (e.g. 100 Gb RDMA ring: \"do this last, scope it for future work\") goes into the handoff doc as an explicit scoped section, never silently omitted.\n- **Name resources after their consumer** — LDAP groups/service accounts carry the VM/hostname (e.g. `llm-manager-vm114-admin`, `svc-llm-manager114`), not generic role names.\n- **UI-as-control-plane (Jordan, 2026-09-10):** feature requests land as /admin dashboard asks by analogy to services he knows (\"token cost like OpenRouter shows\"). Spend analytics (v0.6.0), RDMA ring config option (v0.7.0), and facility rows (v0.7.1) all landed this way — prefer wiring new capabilities into the web UI with supporting endpoints, not CLI-only paths. He personally reviews dashboards and reports what he sees, so keep displayed caveats accurate.\n- **Power telemetry sources (Jordan rule, 2026-09-10):** GPU NVML = the primary dynamic signal; Emporia = independent facility/whole-circuit view; **PDUs are reserved for control — never poll PDU network endpoints for power telemetry** (\"we do not want to block the PDUs with network traffic\"; at most a once-daily spot read). Local-model $/token accounting: GPU watts × effective seasonal electricity rate (componentized records), rolling 7/30-day windows, reported in both GPU-attributed (primary basis) and server-total (high-end margin) variants.\n- **\"Continue until finished; if you hit a roadblock, work on other related tasks\"** — when the user authorizes full-plan execution, keep every phase on the todo list, dispatch independent workstreams in parallel (delegate_task), and never end a turn with only a plan or a partial report. Deferred ≠ stopped. Parallel background builders write progress-note files (e.g. `<topic>-progress.md`) in the session work dir — long remote builds (model downloads, cmake) must run under nohup on the remote machine, and the orchestrator integrates results when builders finish.\n- **Verify \"missing access\" claims with verbose tracing before reporting a roadblock** — an empty-output SSH run was once misread as \"no node access\" when the root password sat in `~/.miam_root_pass` all along; the fix was a better runner, not an escalation to the user (see pitfalls).\n- **Hashing ≠ encryption — say so when the user sees crypto tooling.** The user asked \"we don't want to encrypt any of this storage stuff... can you explain what you're doing?\" when bcrypt/argon2 appeared mid-build. Rule: when a password hash, TLS cert, or sealed-secret step shows up in output, add one plain-language line (\"this is a login check, not storage encryption; nothing on disk is encrypted; no performance impact\"). Never let crypto vocabulary pass unexplained — the user reads it as hidden storage-encryption scope creep.\n\n## Web control plane: auth + role mapping (built 2026-09-09)\n\n- **Login session flow**: FastAPI `SessionMiddleware` (key in a 600 secret file) + LDAP\n  bind-login via the service bind account, then verify the user by binding AS the user DN.\n  Role = strongest `llm-manager-vm114-*` group. Write APIs gated on role sets\n  (Operator→restarts; Admin/Security-Admin→key issuance + unit edits).\n- **nginx route table must enumerate EVERY route the app serves** — `/login`, `/logout`,\n  `/admin`, `/api/`, `/healthz` — anything unlisted falls into `location / → 302 /admin` and\n  produces an infinite redirect loop (browser: ERR_TOO_MANY_REDIRECTS; tests: 302 chains).\n  After adding app routes, `nginx -t && systemctl reload nginx`.\n- **LiteLLM admin API verbs**: `/key/generate`, `/key/block`, `/key/delete`, `/key/update`\n  are POST, but **`/key/list` AND `/key/info` are GET** (POST returns 405 Method Not Allowed).\n  - `GET /key/list` returns `{\"keys\": [<raw token STRINGS>], \"total_count\": …}` — NOT\n    objects. Iterating with `k.get(...)` throws `'str' object has no attribute 'get'`.\n    Fetch per-key metadata with `GET /key/info?key=<token>` (query param, not JSON body);\n    the useful fields live under `info` (`key_alias`, `spend`, `max_budget`, `blocked`,\n    `expires`, `models`).\n  - Truncated 502 responses from `/key/generate` usually mean LiteLLM can't reach its Postgres — check\n  the litellm journal for `httpx.ConnectError` before touching app code. Then diagnose the\n  unreachable-DB in order: (1) port REFUSED while ICMP/SSH work + service verified\n  listening on the DB CT = **IP collision with a physical host** (compare `ip neigh <ip>`\n  MAC vs the CT's real NIC MAC — this happened: a DB CT sat on a cluster node's corosync\n  address; see proxmox skill's network audit reference), (2) pg_hba/grants only after\n  connectivity is proven.\n- **DB relocation runbook** (moved CT across IPs): grep every consumer for the old IP\n  (`grep -rn \"10.0.20.<old>\" /opt/<app>/ /etc/<app>/`), sed-repoint app `PG_HOST` and\n  gateway `database_url` in the same pass, `systemctl restart` BOTH services, then prove\n  with the service ports (app login page + gateway `/v1/models` 401-with-bad-key = healthy\n  upstream), not just `systemctl is-active`. An unrepointed stale reference survived one\n  move and was only caught during a later network audit.\n- **Audit everything to a JSONL file** (`/var/log/llm-manager/audit.log`): login ok/denied +\n  reason, key issue/block/revoke, unit edits, restarts. The audit log is what makes\n  debugging auth failures possible (\"unknown user\" vs \"invalid credentials\" vs \"no role\"\n  each pinpoint a different broken layer).\n- **Guest control agent pattern** (no arbitrary shell): fixed-command script\n  `/opt/llm-control/llm-control.sh {status|get-unit|set-unit|restart|logs|nvidia}` per vLLM\n  guest; gateway host holds a dedicated SSH keypair (`/etc/llm-manager/secrets/keys/`),\n  pub key appended to each guest's `authorized_keys` via the owning node + base64\n  `qm guest exec` (stdin is NOT forwarded — see proxmox skill). Unit edits: validate\n  `[Service]` + `ExecStart=` present, keep `.bak.<ts>`, `daemon-reload` WITHOUT restart —\n  the UI instructs the operator to restart separately (save ≠ apply).\n- **Keep the relay path documented for remote administration.** ssh_run.py (root+pexpect)\n  reaches Proxmox NODES only; the gateway VM takes a different path: land script on\n  owning node → `scp ubuntu@<vm-ip>` → `ssh … \"sudo bash /tmp/x.sh\"`. A session burned a\n  round-trip trying to ssh_run.py straight into the VM (`Permission denied (publickey)`).\n  Before driving a guest, check which path applies (node = root/password, VM =\n  node-relay with the baked-in key).\n\n## Deployment recipes\n\n- **Never invent vLLM flags.** Recover the battle-tested serve command from the target guest's root `~/.bash_history` (last working variant) and encode it in a systemd unit. Environment: `HF_HOME`, `VLLM_USE_FLASHINFER_SAMPLER=0`, `VLLM_USE_DEEP_GEMM=0` are typical for this fleet.\n- **Per-deployment profiles.** The same logical model name can expose different `max_model_len` (observed 262144 vs 32768 on identical model IDs). Store concurrency/context envelopes per deployment; never assume homogeneity.\n- **Routable requires proof:** `/v1/models` returns the expected model AND a real chat completion succeeds — health endpoint alone is insufficient.\n- Qwen thinking models may emit reasoning inline unless `--reasoning-parser qwen3` is set — check parser flags when output contains \"Thinking Process...\". Thinking traces consume `max_tokens`: a \"max_tokens: 40\" test request can be fully consumed by the reasoning preamble before any answer text appears.\n- **Reasoning-parser fleet rollout pattern (verified 2026-09-10):** canary on ONE host first — add `--reasoning-parser qwen3` next to `--tool-call-parser qwen3_xml` in the unit, restart, verify `/v1/models` after the ~3-4 min reload, THEN confirm (a) `reasoning` field is separated and `content` is clean, (b) tool calling still works (parser combo must return proper `tool_calls`, not raw XML). Only then roll the rest, **staggered with per-host health gates** and automatic rollback if a host fails to come up in ~5 min. Unit NAMES differ per host (`vllm.service` vs `vllm-qwen.service`) — detect the RUNNING unit (`systemctl list-units --state=running | grep vllm`) instead of assuming; grep matching \"already set\" against the wrong unit file gives false positives. Clients get clean content via `\"chat_template_kwargs\": {\"enable_thinking\": false}` (e.g. `content: '27'` end-to-end through the gateway); with thinking ON, max_tokens is consumed by the reasoning preamble first.\n- **Hybrid-Mamba models (Qwen3.x family): `--max-num-seqs` must fit the Mamba cache.** Every concurrent sequence holds one Mamba cache block; 16 GB cards at `--gpu-memory-utilization 0.92` fit only ~92 blocks, so vLLM's default 256 aborts during CUDA-graph capture with `RuntimeError: max_num_seqs (256) exceeds available Mamba cache blocks (92)` and the unit crash-loops. Fix: `--max-num-seqs 64` (comfortable headroom, still generous for Hermes load). Never copy the 3080 hosts' turboquant KV/attention flags onto smaller-VRAM GPUs without qualification.\n- **Crash-loop signature:** systemd reports `active` while `NRestarts` climbs, worker PIDs change between checks, and nothing listens on :8000. Don't keep polling the port — pull `journalctl -u vllm --no-pager | grep -iE \"error|RuntimeError\"` for the root cause directly. First successful boot takes ~4–5 min (weights ≈40 s/rank + torch.compile); the compile cache makes later restarts much faster.\n- **Fleet unit management via node relay:** land scripts on the gateway VM → `scp -i /etc/llm-manager/secrets/keys/id_ed25519` to guests → `ssh root@<guest> \"bash /tmp/x.sh\"` (guests have the control key). Service names differ per host (`vllm.service` vs `vllm-qwen.service`) — always resolve the RUNNING unit dynamically before editing. `--help` on modern vLLM is truncated: use `vllm serve --help=<flag>` (e.g. `--help=reasoning-parser`) for a specific flag's existence.\n- **Re-registering a recovered backend into the gateway pool:** idempotent-edit `/etc/llm-manager/litellm_config.yaml` (skip if the endpoint IP is already present), `systemctl restart litellm`, then PROVE it: `GET /v1/models` with the master key AND one real chat completion through an agent virtual key (expect 200 + usage accounting).\n\n## Manager web-app additive patching (v0.6.0–v0.7.1 pattern, proven 2026-09-10)\n\n- Author patches as standalone python scripts whose every old_string replace is wrapped in `assert X in src` — the script fails loudly instead of silently skipping when the live file drifted.\n- Dry-run BEFORE touching the server: `PATCH_TARGET=<local copy>` env override + run patch locally + `ast.parse` the result + grep the new symbols. Then scp the patch and run `sudo python3 patch.py` on the gateway VM — the app dir is root-owned, so backups must be created by root (ubuntu user gets PermissionError on the backup write).\n- Verify with TestClient + hand-signed session cookie BEFORE `systemctl restart`, then restart and check /healthz + /login + /admin.\n- Escaping traps when injecting code from a triple-quoted patch body: (1) f-string backslash line-continuations (e.g. `vllm serve ... \\`) need `\\\\\\\\` in the patch source — a single `\\\\` collapses to an escaped quote and breaks the generated string; (2) FastAPI snake_case params like `from_` map to query key `from_`, NOT `from` — client JS must send `from_=`; (3) anchor on the literal `§` glyph, never a `\\\\u00a7` escape (previous patches wrote the literal glyph).\n- Structure: bump the APP_VERSION string, insert routes before a stable marker comment, replace dashboard HTML/JS blocks via exact anchors, keep everything additive with a `.bak.<ts>` rollback.\n\n## Benchmarking + qualification envelopes (Phase 8/11, proven 2026-09-10)\n\n- **Streaming gives REAL TTFT** — non-streaming requests can't measure first-token latency; use `\"stream\": true` and timestamp the first `data:` chunk. Benchmark client: thread pool at preset concurrency (C1/C10/C16/C20/C32/C48), 100 requests (20 for C1), fixed pad prompt (~4096 tokens), `temperature: 0`, thinking-off.\n- **Sweep before setting envelopes.** The 5060 Ti host (.162, Mamba-cache-limited) passed C10→C48 with ZERO failures at 4096-in/256-out (354→773 tok/s) — its Mamba ceiling was never reached because `--max-num-seqs 64` protects it; set envelopes conservatively (rec 16 / hard 32) regardless. The 3080 hosts match the plan §3.1 baseline at C10 (254 vs 249.85 tok/s) — treat a C10 run ≥ baseline as validation, then test the hard-limit concurrency (C20) before stamping `hard_concurrency_limit`.\n- Store runs in PG (`benchmark_runs`: preset, concurrency, ok/failed, tok/s, TTFT avg/max, latency avg, target, notes) and the approved envelope in `qualification_envelopes` per deployment IP. Envelope → gateway enforcement v1: per-deployment `rpm:` limits in LiteLLM model_list params (rec_concurrency × ~30 req/min; e.g. 300 rpm for C10 hosts, 480 for the C16 host), then `systemctl restart litellm` and re-verify gateway 200 + one real completion.\n- **Known-good engine script**: `scripts/bench_engine.py` in this skill — self-contained, streaming TTFT, RESULT_JSON line for machine parsing. Copy per session; per-deployment `--deployment http://<ip>:8000` targets one backend directly (bypasses gateway round-robin).\n\n## Power-based spend accounting (proven live 2026-09-10, v0.5.0)\n\n- **Design (user-approved):** energy attributed from GPU NVML watts (primary) with a server-total mode adding per-host platform estimate (`idle_non_gpu_watts=120` + `0.35 W per CPU-util-point` — documented assumptions, refine vs Emporia later). Hex-encode; push through the pexpect node runner to node 135 `/tmp` in ≤40 KB hex\n   chunks with per-chunk `wc -c` verification (pexpect argv limit ≈ 100 KB —\n   `Argument list too long`; raw base64 through the pty corrupted bytes once — always\n   md5-verify the reassembled file).\n3. Reassemble: `cat chunks | python3 -c \"import binascii,sys; open(out,'wb').write(binascii.unhexlify(sys.stdin.read()))\"`.\n4. `scp -o BatchMode=yes` node→node (node 135 holds BatchMode keys to the ring nodes),\n   `dpkg -i`.\niperf3 dependency chain: `iperf3` + `libiperf0` + `libssl1.1` + `libsctp1` (Debian\nbullseye builds run fine on PVE 9 hosts). Server side: `ib_send_bw -d mlx5_1 -R`\n  (background), client: `ib_send_bw -c RC -d mlx5_0 -R <server-ip>` (flag order matters:\n  `-c RC` is the connection type, NOT the address; address goes last, `-R` enables rdma_cm).\n- **Verbs userspace stack** (now installed on .111/.144 via the offline pipeline):\n  `rdma-core` + `libibverbs1` + `ibverbs-providers` + `libibumad3` + `perftest` (provides\n  `ib_send_bw`) + iperf3 chain. Host-side RDMA ≠ guest RDMA. This is the\n  blocking design decision for RDMA-3/4 — needs the user's sign-off on passthrough vs\n  SR-IOV before any guest changes.\n- **RDMA-3/4**: multi-node vLLM functional test → >120 GB model qualification →\n  production routing gate (§19.8). Guest-agent exec on VM103 failed with HTTP 596 (agent transport issue via that route). Before the canary, one important insight from the configs: **VM111's efidisk already has `pre-enrolled-keys=0`** (no MS keys → Secure Boot shouldn't even be active there), while **VM103 has `pre-enrolled-keys=1`** (MS keys enrolled → SB on). So the smart order is:

1. **Start VM111** (it's stopped — zero-risk) and check its SB/lockdown state from inside
2. Learn from that whether `pre-enrolled-keys=0` gives us what we need
3. Then handle VM103 (needs the efidisk recreation procedure)

---

### [2026-09-10T18:36:21.235542+00:00] tool · tool: patch

{"success": true, "diff": "--- a//home/jordatech/work/pve_api.py\n+++ b//home/jordatech/work/pve_api.py\n@@ -33,7 +33,11 @@\n \n \n def raw_req(method, path, ticket, csrf, body=None):\n-    data = urllib.parse.urlencode(body).encode() if isinstance(body, dict) else body\n+    data = None\n+    if isinstance(body, dict):\n+        data = urllib.parse.urlencode(body, doseq=True).encode()\n+    elif isinstance(body, str):\n+        data = body.encode()\n     r = urllib.request.Request(BASE + path, data=data, method=method)\n     if ticket:\n         r.add_header(\"Cookie\", \"PVEAuthCookie=\" + ticket)\n", "files_modified": ["/home/jordatech/work/pve_api.py"], "lint": {"status": "ok", "output": ""}, "resolved_path": "/home/jordatech/work/pve_api.py"}

### [2026-09-10T18:55:09.655213+00:00] assistant

Root cause is now solid: **mainline `ib_core` on kernel 6.8 has no peer-memory-provider API at all** (787 exports, zero containing "peer"), and Ubuntu's `nvidia-peermem.ko` is an 8 KB hollow stub that imports nothing and fails at init. Secure Boot removal (done on VM111) was necessary but not sufficient — on stock Ubuntu kernels, peermem can never load regardless.

That opens the key question: NCCL 2.18+ has an alternate GDR path via **DMA-BUF registration (`NCCL_DMABUF_ENABLE=1`)** that doesn't need peermem at all. Testing that now — first checking both guests' ring interfaces and existing NCCL test assets:

### [2026-09-10T19:04:43.851614+00:00] assistant

**Root cause found and fixed: VM103's ring port had reset to MTU 1500** (the post-reboot re-plumb I didn't complete on that side) — the MTU mismatch on the wire caused every QP transfer to fail with access-violation/flush errors, masquerading as a GPU-direct problem. With both sides at MTU 9000 + explicit `-m 4096`, verbs blast **11,667 MB/s ≈ 93.3 Gbit/s** — full speed restored. Now re-running the 2-node NCCL all_reduce (staged mode, no DMABUF):

---

## Session Report — RDMA workstream

**Recovered & re-qualified ✅**
- **VM111/.164 was found STOPPED** (left mid-attempt last session) — restarted, hit the documented OOM-orphan pitfall, cleared it, and also found + removed a **duplicate `vllm.service`** crash-looping against the canonical `vllm-qwen.service` (57 restarts!). Fleet is back to 4 healthy backends, real completion verified on .164.
- **Staged RDMA re-proven end-to-end**: verbs 11,667 MB/s ≈ 93.3 Gbit/s, 2-node NCCL ALLREDUCE **PASS** over NET/IB RC (RoCEv2 GID 1). Registry updated with evidence key `rdma2_reproof_2026_09_10`.

**The GDR verdict (important, changes the plan):**
1. Secure Boot disabled on VM111 (verified Setup Mode) — the interrupted step did land.
2. **`nvidia-peermem` still failed EINVAL → root cause found: it was never Secure Boot.** Ubuntu's peermem module is an 8 KB hollow stub (3 symbol imports, zero ib/NVIDIA references) and the stock-kernel `ib_core` exports **no peer-memory-provider API at all**. GDR-via-peermem is impossible on this OS build — I left VM103's SB **on** since disabling it buys nothing.
3. DMABUF path tested fairly (`NCCL_DMABUF_ENABLE=1` + `NCCL_NET_GDR_LEVEL=SYS`): accepted, collectives pass, but NCCL keeps `GDR 0` — the virtualized PCIe fabric can't do it. **Conclusion: GDR on this stack needs either MLNX_OFED-class ib_core in the guests or bare-metal inference hosts.** Documented in skill + registry so nobody re-chases this.

**A trap worth flagging:** VM103's ring port had silently reset to **MTU 1500** after reboot — that produces QP access-violation errors that masquerade *exactly* like a GPU-direct failure. Fixed to 9000; now noted as a standard pre-flight check.

**Procurement spec for 143:** 2× **QSFP-100G-CU2M** (100G-CR4-rated QSFP28 DAC, 2m — match the working leg's part). Swapping those unblocks RDMA qualification of MIAM-00143 and the full 3-node ring.

Still running in background: OpenRouter caps audit + peermem/kernel research (results will land here when done).

Everything's recorded. Here's the closing picture for this push:

## §9.3 Caps — Fixed and Proven

**The audit's verdict:** your $5/$50/$3 caps were *configured* but *decorative* — no code anywhere compared actual spend to them, no hard-stop existed, and the OpenRouter path was always reachable regardless of the `fallback_enabled` flag. Plan §23.7 says fallback without enforcement is a violation, so I closed it.

**Deployed:** `spend_guard.py` — a LiteLLM callback on VM114 that, for any request routed to an OpenRouter deployment only:
- reads caps from `manager_settings` (30 s cache — UI changes apply without restarts)
- sums real spend from `LiteLLM_SpendLogs` (day/month windows)
- **429 hard-stop at daily or monthly cap**, journal WARN at the $3 threshold
- **fails closed** if caps/spend can't be read (local traffic never affected)

**Live proof:** temporarily set the daily cap to $0.000001 + iptables-blocked the local backends → fallback fired → guard rejected it with *"429: OpenRouter daily spend cap reached"* → restored $5/$50/$3 + backends verified 200. Audit entry logged. Skill + handoff updated.

It's the `vm906_llm_manager_fallback` key; after rotating, stage the new one and I'll swap it into `litellm.env` + `manager_settings` following the rotation runbook.

{"success": true, "name": "proxmox-cluster-infrastructure", "file": "scripts/noderun.py", "content": "#!/usr/bin/env python3\n\"\"\"Drain-hardened password-SSH runner for Proxmox MIAM nodes (pexpect).\n\nSuccessor to scripts/ssh_run.py: fixes the empty-output failure mode where a single\nexpect(EOF) + child.before read returned truncated/empty output. This runner:\n  1. spawns ssh, waits for the [Pp]assword: prompt, sends the credential\n  2. feeds the script as base64 (no quoting/PTY-mangling issues for the script TEXT)\n  3. loops expect([password, END-marker, EOF, TIMEOUT]), appending child.before on\n     EVERY iteration until the END marker or EOF\n  4. flags auth failure only on a SECOND password prompt or \"Permission denied\"\n     (never on a literal `cipassword: ****` line from qm config output)\n\nReads the credential from /home/jordatech/.miam_root_pass (root password shared by the\nMIAM Proxmox nodes AND the OPNsense web UI). Never prints it.\n\nUsage (module):   from noderun import run; run(\"10.0.20.135\", script_text, timeout=120)\nUsage (CLI):      python3 noderun.py <host> <script-file.sh>\n\"\"\"\nimport base64\nimport sys\nimport time\n\nimport pexpect\n\nEND = \"___RUN_DONE_42___\"\n\n\ndef run(host: str, script: str, user: str = \"root\", timeout: int = 120) -> str:\n    pw = open(\"/home/jordatech/.miam_root_pass\").read().strip()\n    b64 = base64.b64encode(script.encode()).decode()\n    wrapped = f\"echo {b64} | base64 -d | bash; echo {END}\"\n    child = pexpect.spawn(\n        \"/usr/bin/ssh\",\n        [\"-o\", \"StrictHostKeyChecking=no\", \"-o\", \"UserKnownHostsFile=/dev/null\",\n         \"-o\", \"ConnectTimeout=10\", \"-l\", user, host, wrapped],\n        encoding=\"utf-8\", timeout=timeout, maxread=65536)\n    pieces = []\n    sent = False\n    deadline = time.time() + timeout\n    while time.time() < deadline:\n        i = child.expect([r\"[Pp]assword\\s*:\", END, pexpect.EOF, pexpect.TIMEOUT],\n                         timeout=15)\n        if i == 0 and not sent:\n            child.sendline(pw)\n            sent = True\n        elif i == 0 and sent:\n            # a second password prompt after sending = auth failure\n            pieces.append(child.before or \"\")\n            break\n        elif i == 1:\n            pieces.append(child.before or \"\")\n            break\n        elif i == 2:\n            pieces.append(child.before or \"\")\n            break\n        else:\n            pieces.append(child.before or \"\")\n    child.close()\n    text = \"\".join(pieces)\n    text = text.replace(\"\\r\\n\", \"\\n\").replace(\"\\r\", \"\\n\")\n    if \"Permission denied\" in text:\n        text += \"\\n*** AUTH FAILED ***\"\n    return text\n\n\nif __name__ == \"__main__\":\n    host = sys.argv[1]\n    path = sys.argv[2]\n    script = open(path).read()\n    print(run(host, script))\n", "file_type": ".py"}

### [2026-09-10T23:35:12.787912+00:00] assistant

All closed out. Final report for the window:

## RDMA-3 Window Results

**✅ Achieved (functional proof per plan §19.6):**
- Ray 2.58.0 installed on both guests; **2-node Ray cluster UP** over the 100 Gb ring (12 GPUs pooled across VM103+VM111)
- Multi-node vLLM 0.28 served Qwen3-1.7B: `/v1/models` 200 + real completion through the Ray-spanned engine
- Window cost measured and contained: .161 down ~11 min, .164 down ~3 min, **both restored to serving** (180 s / 20 s via compile cache)

**⏸ One step left for the next window — strict cross-node TP:** Ray kept packing both ranks on VM103. Root cause found in vLLM 0.28's installed source: `strategy="PACK"` is hardcoded, and vLLM only adopts a pre-existing placement group when the *driver itself runs inside one*. The fix is documented (run the serve inside `ray job submit` with a pre-created STRICT_SPREAD PG) — ready to execute next window.

## Your MIAM-00143 Question — Answered Definitively

**The cards are identical. DACs remain the problem.**

| | MIAM-00111 | MIAM-00143 | MIAM-00144 |
|---|---|---|---|
| Adapter | ConnectX-5 Ex (MT28800) | ConnectX-5 Ex (MT28800) | ConnectX-5 Ex (15b3:1019) |
| Firmware | 16.35.1012 (DEL0000000004) | **same** | **same** |
| Driver/Kernel | mlx5_core 7.0.14-11-pve | **same** | **same** |

Key evidence: 143's ports **do** train — both legs show link-detected-yes at 1 Gb. A dead card wouldn't train at all. It trains at 1G only because the OEM **QSFP-100G-DAC2M** parts (serials 2603050038/40, EEPROM-identified) are 1G-only unequalized copper. The working leg's genuine 100G-CR4 part is **QSFP-100G-CU2M**. **Card replacement not indicated — order the 2 DACs.**

## On deck

1. 🛒 **DAC order** (now fully justified): 2× QSFP-100G-CU2M
2. 🕐 **RDMA-3 cross-node TP** — next window, design documented
3. 🖥️ **Web UI 3-node RDMA config card** (v0.7.2) — I'll build it after cross-node TP is proven, so the UI generates a config that actually works
4. 🔑 **OpenRouter key rotation** — still on you

import sys, base64, json, time, urllib.parse
sys.path.insert(0, '/home/jordatech/work')
from pve_api import api

def gdispatch(node, vmid, script):
    st, d = api("POST", "/api2/json/nodes/%s/qemu/%s/agent/exec" % (node, vmid),
                body={"command": ["bash","-lc","echo %s | base64 -d | bash" % base64.b64encode(script.encode()).decode()]})
    return (d.get('data') or {}).get('pid')

def gstatus(node, vmid, pid):
    st, d = api("GET", "/api2/json/nodes/%s/qemu/%s/agent/exec-status?pid=%d" % (node, vmid, pid))
    return d.get('data') or {}

# HEAD: vLLM serve on VM103, TP=2 spanning ray cluster; NCCL over ring
head = '''
export HF_HOME=/opt/vllm-cache
export HF_HUB_OFFLINE=1
export NCCL_IB_GID_INDEX=1
export NCCL_SOCKET_IFNAME=enp3s0np1
export VLLM_USE_FLASHINFER_SAMPLER=0
export VLLM_USE_DEEP_GEMM=0
setsid /opt/vllm-venv/bin/vllm serve Qwen/Qwen3-1.7B \\
  --served-model-name qwen3-1.7b-mn \\
  --tensor-parallel-size 2 \\
  --distributed-executor-backend ray \\
  --max-model-len 8192 \\
  --gpu-memory-utilization 0.85 \\
  --host 0.0.0.0 --port 8000 \\
  > /tmp/vllm_mn.log 2>&1 &
echo "head serve launched pid=$!"
'''
p1 = gdispatch("miam00111", 103, head)
print("head serve dispatch:", p1, flush=True)

# poll vllm_mn.log on head for readiness or fatal error, up to ~13 min
ready = False
for i in range(78):
    time.sleep(10)
    st, d = api("GET", "/api2/json/nodes/miam00111/qemu/103/agent/exec")
    # cheap status probe: fetch last lines of remote log via short exec
    probe = "tail -3 /tmp/vllm_mn.log; curl -s -o /dev/null -w '%{http_code}' --max-time 3 http://localhost:8000/v1/models || true"
    p2 = gdispatch("miam00111", 103, probe)
    for _ in range(20):
        st2, d2 = api("GET", "/api2/json/nodes/miam00111/qemu/103/agent/exec-status?pid=%d" % p2)
        dd = d2.get('data') or {}
        if dd.get('exited'):
            out = dd.get('out-data') or ''
            print("[%03d]" % (i*10), out.strip().replace(chr(10), ' | ')[-260:], flush=True)
            if '200' in out.splitlines()[-1] if out.splitlines() else False:
                ready = True
            break
    if ready:
        break

print("READY:", ready, flush=True)
EOF

import sys, base64, json, time, urllib.parse
sys.path.insert(0, '/home/jordatech/work')
from pve_api import api

def gexec(node, vmid, script, timeout=90):
    st, d = api("POST", "/api2/json/nodes/%s/qemu/%s/agent/exec" % (node, vmid),
                body={"command": ["bash","-lc","echo %s | base64 -d | bash" % base64.b64encode(script.encode()).decode()]})
    pid = (d.get('data') or {}).get('pid')
    if not pid:
        return {'dispatch': st, 'resp': d}
    for _ in range(timeout):
        st2, d2 = api("GET", "/api2/json/nodes/%s/qemu/%s/agent/exec-status?pid=%d" % (node, vmid, pid))
        dd = d2.get('data') or {}
        if dd.get('exited'):
            return {'exitcode': dd.get('exitcode'), 'out': (dd.get('out-data') or ''), 'err': (dd.get('err-data') or '')[:300]}
        time.sleep(1)
    return {'error': 'timeout'}

# start canonical units
for node, vmid, ip, unit in [("miam00111","103","161","vllm.service"), ("miam00144","111","164","vllm-qwen.service")]:
    r = gexec(node, vmid, "systemctl reset-failed %s 2>/dev/null; systemctl start %s; systemctl is-active %s" % (unit, unit, unit), timeout=60)
    print("VM%s start %s:" % (ip, unit), (r.get('out') or json.dumps(r)).strip(), flush=True)

# poll both to port-200
def wait_ready(node, vmid, ip):
    for i in range(36):
        time.sleep(10)
        r = gexec(node, vmid, "curl -s -o /dev/null -w '%{http_code}' --max-time 3 http://localhost:8000/v1/models", timeout=40)
        code = (r.get('out') or '').strip()
        print("VM%s [%03d]: %s" % (ip, (i+1)*10, code), flush=True)
        if code == '200':
            return True
    return False

ok1 = wait_ready("miam00111", 103, "103")
ok2 = wait_ready("miam00144", 111, "111")
print("RESTORED:", ok1, ok2, flush=True)
